impl BoundProgram {
    pub fn filter_source(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.filters.get(site).map(String::as_str) }
    pub fn domain_source(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.sources.get(&format!("domain/{site}")).map(|(_, text)| text.as_str()) }
    pub fn loop_source(&self, binding: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.sources.get(&format!("for/{binding}")).map(|(_, text)| text.as_str()) }
    pub fn selections(&self) -> &BTreeMap<String, String> { &self.selections }
    /// Whether the scan at `site` is a timeline read's own expansion, not an authored scan.
    pub fn timeline_scan(&self, site: &str) -> bool { self.timeline_scans.contains(site) }
    /// The actual binder choice, shared by `why` and structural cost reports.
    pub fn selection(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.selections.get(site).map(String::as_str) }
}

#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Binder<'a> {
    viewer_depth: Option<u32>,
    pure_functions: BTreeMap<String, bool>,
    maintenance_scopes: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    call_types: BTreeMap<Vec<String>, Ty>,
    filters: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    view_types: BTreeMap<String, Ty>,
    constraint_domains: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
    sources: BTreeMap<String, (Site, String)>,
    page_sources: BTreeMap<String, ReadExpr>,
    canonical_sources: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    selections: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    presence_sources: Vec<(Expr, Site, String)>,
    receipt_sources: Vec<(Expr, Site)>,
    receipt_returns: Vec<(String, Site)>,
    implicit_write_results: Vec<Expr>,
    catalog: &'a Catalog,
    schema: &'a snapback4_core::schema::Schema,
    name: String,
    kind: ProgramKind,
    caller_kind: ProgramKind,
    /// Diagnostic-only caller path; helpers never change execution authority.
    call_chain: Vec<(String, Site)>,
    job_bound_helpers: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
    scopes: Vec<BTreeMap<String, Binding>>,
    focal: Option<(String, Ty)>,
    /// Only direct scanned-row columns get false-on-null ordering in where.
    in_predicate: bool,
    returned_data: bool,
    shape_field: Option<(String, Site)>,
    variants: Vec<(String, bool)>,
    temps: u32,
    sites: u32,
    returns: Vec<(Ty, A)>,
    /// Depth of helper expansion, to bound recursion.
    depth: u32,
    /// Enclosing `for` and `chunk` bodies: where `break` may stand.
    loops: u32,
    /// Binding facts carried by the current loop's transfer edges. Ordinary
    /// fallthrough remains in the lexical scope; these edges meet their own
    /// destinations at the loop header or exit.
    loop_transfers: Vec<LoopTransfers>,
    /// Enclosing caller loops affect placement, but never helper-local jumps.
    caller_loops: u32,
    in_prelude: bool,
    /// The total being bound (`count`, or `sum` and its column), for E_QUARRY_BOUND's view recipe.
    total: Option<(&'static str, Option<String>)>,
    record_ownership: bool,
    /// The feed table and source site of the timeline read being expanded, while
    /// it is: the generated scan carries that read's own site.
    expanding_feed: Option<(String, Site)>,
    timeline_scans: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
    notes: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    errors: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    /// Totals over partly readable rows that a readable grant may decide:
    /// refused only if the finished program does not guard the scan with it.
    pending_totals: Vec<(String, Diagnostic)>,
}

/// Source names are lexical; the IR's flat environment needs a distinct
/// runtime name for each declaration and each helper expansion.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Binding {
    runtime: String,
    ty: Ty,
    declared: Ty,
    parameter: bool,
    revision: u32,
    fields: BTreeMap<Vec<String>, Ty>,
    /// An invalid binding retains its original cause; its unknown type never escapes compilation.
    error: Option<Diagnostic>,
    /// Original operands for a diagnostic rewrite, never type or runtime provenance.
    origins: Vec<(Ty, A)>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Default)]
struct LoopTransfers {
    /// Binding state is keyed by runtime identity so a same-named loop item
    /// cannot hide an outer local from the header or exit meet.
    breaks: Vec<BTreeMap<String, Binding>>,
    continues: Vec<BTreeMap<String, Binding>>,
}

    /// An expression in an identity position: a text literal becomes an id.
    pub(super) fn expr_as_identity(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } = expr {
            if text.is_empty() {
                return Err(type_error(expr.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), expected.required()));
        }
        self.expr(expr, out)
    }

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
            }
        }
        (value, ty)
    }

    fn parse_use(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        let site = self.take("use")?.site;
        let kind = self.word()?;
        match kind.value.as_str() {
            "server" => self.document.server = true,
            "identity" => {
                let mut password = true;
                let mut guests = true;
                if self.at("password") {
                    self.position += 1;
                    guests = false;
                } else if self.at("guests") {
                    self.position += 1;
                    password = false;
                }
                if let (Some(first), Some(first_site)) =
                    (self.document.identity, self.document.identity_site.as_ref())
                {
                    return Err(repeated_identity(&site, first, first_site, (password, guests)));
                }
                self.document.identity = Some((password, guests));
                self.document.identity_site = Some(site.clone());
            }
            "personas" => {
                let first = self.word()?;
                self.document.personas.push(first.value);
                while self.maybe(",") {
                    let next = self.word()?;
                    self.document.personas.push(next.value);
                }
            }
            other => return Err(unsupported(&kind.site, &format!("use {other}"), "use server, use identity, use identity password, use identity guests, or use personas alice, bob")),
        }
        self.end_line(&site)
    }
pub fn bind(documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut bound = bind_unverified(documents.clone());
    crate::repair::verify_bound(&documents, &mut bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}

// Repair checks run the same planner and admission, without recursively
// verifying the diagnostics from a rejected candidate.
pub(crate) fn bind_unverified(mut documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    let mut first_identity = None;
    for document in &documents {
        if let (Some(methods), Some(site)) = (document.identity, document.identity_site.as_ref()) {
            if let Some((first_methods, first_site)) = &first_identity {
                diagnostics.push(crate::parser::repeated_identity(
                    site,
                    *first_methods,
                    first_site,
                    methods,
                ));
            } else {
                first_identity = Some((methods, site.clone()));
            }
        }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — catalog provenance comes from source, before any generated declarations.
    let authored_indexes = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc|
        doc.tables.iter().map(|t| (&t.name, &t.indexes))
            .chain(doc.maintains.iter().map(|m| (&m.name, &m.indexes))))
        .map(|(name, indexes)| (name.clone(), indexes.iter().map(|i| i.name.clone()).collect())).collect();
    receipts::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics);
    helper_names::normalize(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let server = documents.iter().any(|doc| doc.server);
    server::admit(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let feeds = feed_view::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
//! Reserved words: Quarry keywords cannot name a column or a binding. The
//! refusal names the word at its declaration, before a later use fails.

use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// Words that begin a table line (`by`, `read <-`, `after insert`, …).
pub(crate) const TABLE_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "after", "by", "cap", "delete", "deliver", "ephemeral", "expire", "immutable", "insert", "online",
    "public", "read", "retain", "search", "sync", "unique", "update",
];

/// Words a statement or expression reads as syntax, so a parameter, local or
/// loop variable of that name cannot be used (or silently means the keyword).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — commit/new/now/viewer are contextual builtins, so declarations cannot shadow them.
pub(crate) const BINDING_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "and", "as", "break", "commit", "continue", "created", "delete", "device", "elif", "else", "emit",
    "exists", "false", "for", "if", "insert", "merge", "new", "not", "now", "null", "oblivious", "or",
    "require", "return", "true", "update", "upsert", "viewer", "walk", "while",
];

fn rename(word: &str) -> String {
    match word {
        "read" => "isRead".into(),
        "by" => "author".into(),
        "after" => "afterAt".into(),
        "update" => "change".into(),
        "delete" => "removal".into(),
        "insert" => "addition".into(),
        "public" => "isPublic".into(),
        "unique" => "isUnique".into(),
        "sync" => "synced".into(),
        "cap" => "capacity".into(),
        "expire" => "expiresAt".into(),
        "not" => "startsAt".into(),
        "online" => "isOnline".into(),
        "search" => "searchText".into(),
        "retain" => "retained".into(),
        "immutable" => "locked".into(),
        "ephemeral" => "transient".into(),
        "deliver" => "delivery".into(),
        "walk" => "outing".into(),
        "viewer" => "who".into(),
        "now" => "at".into(),
        "commit" => "committedAt".into(),
        "new" => "newId".into(),
        other => format!("{other}Value"),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn column(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word: it begins a table line (`{}`), so it cannot name a column", usage(word)),
        format!("rename the column, for example `{name}:`, and use `.{name}` in rules and queries (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn binding(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word, so it cannot name a parameter, local or loop variable"),
        format!("rename it, for example `{name}`, at this declaration and at each use (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
    site: &Site,
    first: (bool, bool),
    first_site: &Site,
    repeated: (bool, bool),
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
        (true, true) => "password and guests",
        (true, false) => "password",
        (false, true) => "guests",
        (false, false) => "no methods",
    };
    Diagnostic::new(
        site,
        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
    )
    .guide("production")
}

fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
    match word {
        "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",
        "by" | "unique" => "by name: columns, unique columns",
        "after" => "after insert <- exists …",
        "public" => "public 'why'",
        "sync" | "online" => "sync … / online only",
        "cap" => "cap N by column",
        "expire" => "expire at .column",
        "not" => "not before .column",
        "immutable" => "immutable columns",
        "retain" => "retain until-revoked",
        "ephemeral" => "ephemeral 5s",
        "search" => "search column",
        _ => "a table line",
    }
}

    /// A parameter, local or loop variable: a keyword cannot be one.
    fn local_name(&mut self) -> Fallible<String> {
        if self.peek().kind == Kind::Word && reserved::BINDING_WORDS.contains(&self.peek().value.as_str()) {
            let token = self.peek().clone();
            return Err(reserved::binding(&token.site, &token.value));
        }
        self.binding_name()
    }

    fn binding_name(&mut self) -> Fallible<String> {
        let token = self.word()?;
        if token.value == "until-revoked" || token.value == "delivered-history" {
            return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "retention policy words cannot name bindings", "use an identifier without a hyphen"));
        }
        Ok(token.value)
    }

        let mut body = Vec::new();
        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) {
                break;
            }
            match self.parse_statement() {
                Ok(statement) => body.push(statement),
                Err(diagnostic) => {
                    self.diagnostics.push(diagnostic);
                    self.recover_line();
                }
            }
        }
        self.take_kind(Kind::Dedent)?;
        Ok(body)
    }

    fn parse_statement(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        if token.kind == Kind::Word && self.peek_at(1).value == "=" && self.peek_at(2).value != "=" && reserved::BINDING_WORDS.contains(&token.value.as_str()) {
            return Err(reserved::binding(&token.site, &token.value));
        }
        match token.value.as_str() {
            "while" => return Err(unsupported(&token.site, "while", "use a bounded for domain or walk")),
            "break" => {
                let site = self.take("break")?.site;
                self.newline()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::Break { site });
            }
            "continue" => {
                let site = self.take("continue")?.site;
                self.newline()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::Continue { site });
            }
            "if" => return self.parse_if(),
            "elif" => return Err(unsupported(&token.site, "elif", "else if cond:")),
            "as" => {
                let site = self.take("as")?.site;
                let principal = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
                self.take(":")?;
                self.newline()?;
                let body = self.parse_block()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::As { principal, body, site });
            }
            "for" => return self.parse_for(),
            "require" => return self.parse_require(),
            "return" => {
                let site = self.take("return")?.site;
                if self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
                    self.newline()?;
                    return Ok(Stmt::Return { value: Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, site: site.clone() }, site });
                }
                let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
                if !matches!(value, Expr::Merge(_)) {
                    self.newline()?;
            return Ok(Stmt::Bind { names, value, site });
        }
        let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
        if !matches!(value, Expr::Merge(_)) {
            self.newline()?;
        }
        let site = value.site().clone();
        Ok(Stmt::Expr { value, site })
    }

    fn parse_if(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let site = self.take("if")?.site;
        let condition = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
        self.take(":")?;
        self.newline()?;
        let then_body = self.parse_block()?;
        let mut else_body = Vec::new();
        if self.at("else") {
            self.take("else")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — else if is a nested conditional with the same scope and flow rules.
            if self.at("if") {
                else_body.push(self.parse_if()?);
            } else {
                self.take(":")?;
                self.newline()?;
                else_body = self.parse_block()?;
            }
        } else if self.at("elif") {
            return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site, "elif", "else if cond:"));
        }
        Ok(Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, site })
    }

    fn parse_for(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let site = self.take("for")?.site;
        let name = self.local_name()?;
        self.take("in")?;
        let domain = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
        self.take(":")?;
        self.newline()?;
        let body = self.parse_block()?;
        Ok(Stmt::For { name, domain, body, site })
    }

   642	        let searching = mode == ReadMode::Range && read.matching.is_some();
   643	        let mut candidates: Vec<&ast::IndexDecl> = info
   644	            .indexes
   645	            .iter()
//! E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR: a table rule uses an expression rules do not
//! evaluate (an ordered comparison outside insert rules and named update
//! transitions, the clock, arithmetic or a helper call). The rewrite moves the
//! author's own comparison into the mutation as `require … else CODE`.

use crate::ast::Expr;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// What the rule language lacks for this term, if anything.
pub(super) fn unsupported_side(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "now" => Some("the clock (`now`)"),
        Expr::Call { .. } => Some("a helper or function call"),
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } if matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "%" | "??") => Some("arithmetic"),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The effect a rule judges, as the author spelled it.
pub(super) fn effect_word(insert: bool, update: bool, read: bool) -> &'static str {
    if insert { "insert" } else if update { "update" } else if read { "read" } else { "delete" }
}

/// The refusal for one comparison `cmp` in a `table`'s `effect` rule.
pub(super) fn diagnostic(site: &Site, table: &str, effect: &str, transition: bool, cmp: &Expr, what: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let code = format!("INVALID_{}", screaming(&field));
    let message = format!("`{text}` in the {effect} rule of {table} uses {what}, which table rules do not evaluate: rules compare columns, viewer and literals with = and !=, and order (<, <=, >, >=) only an insert row's own required int, time, decimal or money columns and integer literals (minor units beside decimal or money), or old/next in a named update (old, next) rule");
    // The mutation names the stored row `row` and supplies new values as arguments.
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    let inserted = |_: Option<&str>, name: &str| name.to_owned();
    let rewrite = match effect {
        "insert" => {
            let mutation = map_fields(&text, &inserted);
            if what == "an ordered comparison" {
                format!("compare this row's own required int or time columns or an integer literal in the insert rule, or check it in the mutation that inserts the row: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            } else {
                format!("check it in the mutation that inserts the row, using the values it inserts: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            }
        }
        "update" if !transition && what == "an ordered comparison" => {
            let next = map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}"));
            format!("name both images and bound the new one: `update (old, next) <- {next}`; or check it in the mutation: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored))
        }
        "read" if what.contains("now") => match direct_focal_field(cmp) {
            Some(expires) => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: hide rows past this row's stored time with `expire at .{expires}`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
            None => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: copy the deciding deadline into a required time column on {table} and use `expire at .expiresAt`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`); `expire at` accepts a local time column, not a reference hop", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        },
        "read" => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot compare magnitudes: filter the query instead (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        _ => format!("check it in the mutation before the write: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored)),
    };
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR", message, rewrite).guide("rules")
}

/// An unnamed `update <- R` holds for the row before and after the write, so
/// its named form is `update (old, next) <- R(old) and R(next)`: every clause
/// kept, the ordered one included (lane 062). The mutation form follows.
pub(crate) fn named_update(table: &str, rule: &Expr, comparison: &str) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let wrap = |s: String| if text.contains(" or ") { format!("({s})") } else { s };
    let old = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("old.{name}")));
    let next = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}")));
    let field = first_field(comparison).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let _ = table;
    format!("name both images; an unnamed update rule holds before and after the write, so the named form keeps both: `update (old, next) <- {old} and {next}`; or drop `{comparison}` from the rule and check the new value in the mutation: `require {} else INVALID_{}`", map_fields(comparison, &|_, name| name.to_owned()), screaming(&field))
}

/// Pasteable named form for a rule that already names one image. Bare focal
/// fields in a named transition refer to the new image.
pub(crate) fn named_update_source(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — preserve authored old/next images and map bare fields to next in named transitions.
    let text = crate::print::expression(rule);
    let body = map_fields(&text, &|image, name| match image {
        Some(image) => format!("{image}.{name}"),
        None => format!("next.{name}"),
    });
    format!("update (old, next) <- {body}")
}

/// A named transition's comparison the rule language cannot type, as the
/// mutation check that states the same condition.
pub(crate) fn transition_require(table: &str, cmp: &Expr) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    format!("check it in the mutation that updates the row: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else INVALID_{}` (the new values are the mutation's arguments)", map_fields(&text, &stored), screaming(&field))
}

/// A table rule's predicate as the check a mutation states before writing:
/// the row's fields become the values the mutation writes.
pub(crate) fn mutation_check(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    map_fields(&strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule)), &|_, name| name.to_owned())
}

/// `else CODE` after a table rule: the refusal of a rule is E_RULE.
pub(crate) fn rule_else(else_site: &Site, effect: &str, rule: &Expr, code: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let code = if code.chars().next().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_uppercase()) { code } else { "NOT_ALLOWED" };
    Diagnostic::new(else_site, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("a table rule takes no `else`: a write its {effect} rule refuses is E_RULE; only `require … else CODE` and `unique … else CODE` name a refusal"),
        format!("keep `{effect} <- {text}`, and name the refusal in the mutation before the write: `require {} else {code}`", mutation_check(rule))).guide("rules")
}

fn strip_parens(text: &str) -> String {
    let trimmed = text.trim();
    if trimmed.starts_with('(') && trimmed.ends_with(')') {
        // Only strip a pair that encloses the whole text.
        let mut depth = 0i32;
        for (i, c) in trimmed.char_indices() {
            match c { '(' => depth += 1, ')' => depth -= 1, _ => {} }
            if depth == 0 && i + 1 < trimmed.len() { return trimmed.to_owned(); }
        }
        return trimmed[1..trimmed.len() - 1].to_owned();
    }
    trimmed.to_owned()
}

fn ident(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' }
fn ident_start(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_' }

/// The first column the comparison names: `.stars`, `old.highBid` or `next.at`.
fn first_field(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    for (i, c) in chars.iter().enumerate() {
        if *c == '.' && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            return Some(chars[i + 1..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect());
        }
    }
    None
}

/// A time field owned by the focal row. Do not descend through a member
/// chain: `.hearing.releaseAt` must never become `expire at .hearing`.
fn direct_focal_field(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => Some(name),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => direct_focal_field(left).or_else(|| direct_focal_field(right)),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => direct_focal_field(value),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Rewrite each focal field `.f`, `old.f` and `next.f` through `map(image, f)`,
/// leaving other member accesses alone.
fn map_fields(text: &str, map: &dyn Fn(Option<&str>, &str) -> String) -> String {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    let mut out = String::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let name_at = |at: usize| -> String { chars[at..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect() };
    while i < chars.len() {
        let c = chars[i];
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — field-looking text inside a literal is data, not a row-image reference.
        // Field-looking text inside a literal is data, not syntax. Preserve
        // the original spelling, including escaped quote characters.
        if matches!(c, '\'' | '"') {
            let quote = c;
            out.push(c);
            i += 1;
            while i < chars.len() {
                let literal = chars[i];
                out.push(literal);
                i += 1;
                if literal == '\\' && i < chars.len() {
                    out.push(chars[i]);
                    i += 1;
                } else if literal == quote {
                    break;
                }
            }
            continue;
        }
        let previous = if i == 0 { None } else { Some(chars[i - 1]) };
        if c == '.' && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != ')' && p != ']') && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            let name = name_at(i + 1);
            out.push_str(&map(None, &name));
            i += 1 + name.chars().count();
            continue;
        }
        if ident(c) && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != '.') {
            let word = name_at(i);
            let after = i + word.chars().count();
            if matches!(word.as_str(), "old" | "next") && chars.get(after) == Some(&'.') && chars.get(after + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
                let name = name_at(after + 1);
                out.push_str(&map(Some(&word), &name));
                i = after + 1 + name.chars().count();
                continue;
            }
            out.push_str(&word);
            i = after;
            continue;
        }
        out.push(c);
        i += 1;
    }
    out
}

fn screaming(name: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (i, c) in name.chars().enumerate() {
        if c.is_ascii_uppercase() && i > 0 { out.push('_'); }
        out.push(c.to_ascii_uppercase());
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fields_move_into_the_mutation() {
        let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".stars >= 1 and .stars <= 5", &|_, name| name.to_owned()), "stars >= 1 and stars <= 5");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("next.highBid >= old.highBid + old.increment", &stored), "highBid >= row.highBid + row.increment");
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".deadline > now", &stored), "row.deadline > now");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("helper(.a, viewer)", &stored), "helper(row.a, viewer)");
        assert_eq!(first_field("(old.highBid + 1)"), Some("highBid".into()));
        assert_eq!(screaming("drawAt"), "DRAW_AT");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a >= 1)"), ".a >= 1");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a) + (.b)"), "(.a) + (.b)");
    }
}
use snapback4_lang::compile;

#[test]
fn project_builtins_are_reserved_for_parameters_locals_and_loop_bindings() {
    for (source, name) in [
        ("query q(now: time):\n  return now\n", "now"),
        ("mutation m():\n  now = 1\n  return now\n", "now"),
        ("mutation m():\n  commit = now\n  return commit\n", "commit"),
        ("mutation m():\n  for new in range(0, 1):\n    return new\n", "new"),
        ("query q(viewer: principal):\n  return viewer\n", "viewer"),
        ("fn helper(viewer: principal):\n  return viewer\n", "viewer"),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        let diagnostic = bound
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .find(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_RESERVED")
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name}: {:?}", bound.diagnostics));
        assert!(diagnostic.message.contains(&format!("`{name}`")), "{diagnostic}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn repeated_identity_declarations_name_both_method_sets() {
    let same_file = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        "use identity password\nuse identity guests\n".into(),
    )]);
    assert_eq!(same_file.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", same_file.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &same_file.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.code, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.line, 2);
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("password") && diagnostic.message.contains("guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );

    let across_files = compile(&[
        ("schema.q".into(), "use identity guests\n".into()),
        ("accounts.q".into(), "use identity\n".into()),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(across_files.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", across_files.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &across_files.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.file, "accounts.q");
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("guests")
            && diagnostic.message.contains("password and guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );
}

fn diagnostic(source: &str, code: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Diagnostic {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    bound
        .diagnostics
        .iter()
        .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code)
        .cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
}

fn replace_and_compile(source: &str, authored: &str, replacement: &str) {
    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, replacement, 1);
    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture does not contain {authored:?}");
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn sql_habits_name_quarry_order_and_emit_compiling_rewrites() {
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { value_nodes(left, visit); value_nodes(right, visit); },
            Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { value_nodes(item, visit); } },
            Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &field.value { value_nodes(value, visit); } } },
            Expr::Shape { base, fields: shape, .. } => { value_nodes(base, visit); fields(shape, visit); },
            Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { value_nodes(then, visit); value_nodes(condition, visit); value_nodes(otherwise, visit); },
            Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
                for key in keys { value_nodes(&key.value, visit); }
                for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &field.value { value_nodes(value, visit); } }
                if let Some(value) = condition { value_nodes(value, visit); }
            },
            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
                if let Some(cursor) = &merge.cursor { value_nodes(cursor, visit); }
                for lane in &merge.lanes {
                    for value in &lane.prefix { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some((_, value)) = &lane.loop_ { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some(value) = &lane.distinct { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some(shape) = &lane.project { fields(shape, visit); }
                    for source in lane.planned.iter().chain(lane.nulls.as_ref().map(|(_, source)| source)) { read(source, false, visit); }
                }
            },
            Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
        }
        visit(value);
    }
    fn input_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
    }
    fn expression_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        if observe_prefix {
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
        } else {
            // Ordinary query values share the input-value boundary: point and
            // prefix probes in row-dependent candidate filters are visible hits.
            value_nodes(&copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, false, visiting, notes));
        }
    }
    fn invalidate(names: &[String], known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        known.retain(|fact| !fact.split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() && c != '_').any(|word| names.iter().any(|name| name == word)));
    }
    fn body(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, statements: &[Stmt], mut known: BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, guard_names: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => {
                    let writes = effectful(catalog, condition);
                    if writes { known.clear(); }
                    for read in absence(condition, false) {
                        guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { site: read.site.clone(), read }, &known, system, notes);
                    }
                    // Calls can return an existence value even when no probe is spelled here.
                    let mut copy = analysis_expr(condition);
                    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
                        if let Expr::Shape { base, .. } = expr {
                            input_guards(catalog, schema, program, base, &known, global, system, visiting, notes);
                        }
                        for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                            // In a requirement, a helper can invert an input
                            // solely in its returned expression. Inspect all
                            // actual values, not just guard-consuming definitions.
                            let inputs = match expr {
                                Expr::Call { args, .. } => args.clone(),
                                Expr::ShapeUse { base, .. } | Expr::Shape { base, .. } => vec![(**base).clone()],
                                _ => vec![],
                            };
                            for input in inputs { input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, &known, global, system, visiting, notes); }
                            let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                            if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { continue; }
                            if let Some(inner) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                                let entry = if let Expr::Call { args, .. } = expr && !shape { call_facts(catalog, &name, args, &known, global) } else { known.intersection(global).cloned().collect() };
                                body(catalog, schema, program, &inner, entry, global, &guard_bindings(catalog, &inner), system, true, visiting, notes);
                            }
                            visiting.remove(&key);
                        }
                    });
                    if !writes { facts(condition, &mut known); }
                }
                Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => {
                    let writes = effectful(catalog, condition);
                    if writes { known.clear(); }
                    guard(catalog, schema, program, condition, &known, system, notes);
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, condition, &known, global, system, true, visiting, notes);
                    let mut yes = known.clone(); if !writes { facts(condition, &mut yes); }
                    let yes = body(catalog, schema, program, then_body, yes, global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    let no = body(catalog, schema, program, else_body, known.clone(), global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    known = yes.intersection(&no).cloned().collect();
                }
                Stmt::For { body: inner, domain, name, .. } => {
                    if effectful(catalog, domain) { known.clear(); }
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, domain, &known, global, system, observe_prefix || guard_names.contains(name), visiting, notes);
                    // Any later iteration can begin after an earlier iteration's helper/write.
                    let (writes, binds) = effects(catalog, inner, &mut BTreeSet::new());
                    let mut changed = BTreeSet::new(); if binds { rebound_names(inner, &mut changed); }
                    let changed = changed.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
                    let mut entry = if writes { BTreeSet::new() } else { known.clone() };
                    invalidate(&changed, &mut entry);
                    invalidate(std::slice::from_ref(name), &mut entry);
                    let after = body(catalog, schema, program, inner, entry, global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    // Break/continue paths may bypass evidence near the body's end.
                    if writes { known.clear(); } else if binds { invalidate(&changed, &mut known); } else { known = known.intersection(&after).cloned().collect(); }
                }
                // Viewer scopes differ from the surrounding principal; never carry its facts in.
                Stmt::As { body: inner, principal, .. } => {
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, principal, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    body(catalog, schema, program, inner, BTreeSet::new(), global, guard_names, false, true, visiting, notes); known.clear();
                }
                Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => break,
                _ => {
                    // Values can contain conditional guards too; writes invalidate earlier role facts.
                    let one = vec![statement.clone()];
                    let wrote = effects(catalog, &one, &mut BTreeSet::new()).0;
                    // Operand order can hide the grant before a later condition in this expression.
                    if wrote && !matches!(statement, Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } if ordered_call(catalog, value)) { known.clear(); }
                    match statement {
                        Stmt::Bind { value, names, .. } => expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix || names.iter().any(|name| guard_names.contains(name)), visiting, notes),
                        Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes),
                        Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                            expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, payload, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                            if let Some(value) = once { expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes); }
                        }
                        _ => {}
                    }
                    if wrote { known.clear(); }
                    else if let Stmt::Bind { names, .. } = statement {
                        invalidate(names, &mut known);
                        if let Stmt::Bind { value, .. } = statement { lookup_fact(catalog, schema, names, value, &mut known); }
                    }
                    if matches!(statement, Stmt::Return { .. }) { break; }
                }
            }
        }
        known
    }
    let mut global = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut tested = Vec::new();
    for program in programs { conditions(&program.prelude, &mut tested); conditions(&program.body, &mut tested); }
    for helper in catalog.functions.values() { if let FunctionBody::Block(body) = &helper.body { conditions(body, &mut tested); } }
    for condition in tested {
        let mut parts = Vec::new(); super::intent::split(condition, &mut parts);
        for part in parts {
            let mut part = analysis_expr(&part);
            let text = fact_text(&part);
            let mut stable = true;
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut part, &mut |expr| {
                if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name != "viewer")
                    || matches!(expr, Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Call { .. } | Expr::Write { .. } | Expr::Shape { .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { .. }) { stable = false; }
            });
            if stable { global.insert(text); }
        }
    }
    for program in programs {
        let system = program.kind == ProgramKind::Job;
        let mut visiting = BTreeSet::new();
        let mut statements = program.prelude.clone(); statements.extend(program.body.iter().cloned());
        let guarded = guard_bindings(catalog, &statements);
        body(catalog, schema, &program.name, &statements, BTreeSet::new(), &global, &guarded, system, program.kind != ProgramKind::Query, &mut visiting, notes);
    }
}

//! Seeks and row-local scan predicates.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.001#4 — retain the binder's actual choice for why, never infer a new lookup or rewrite.
    pub(super) fn record_selection(&mut self, site: &str, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, result: &str, reason: &str) {
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let bindings = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(position, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(position) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let origin = if self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())) { "synthesized; " } else { "" };
        let source = if origin.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; read: {}", self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(read))) };
        let mut selection = format!("selected {} ({bindings}); result: {result}; {origin}{reason}{source}", index.name);
        if !self.variants.is_empty() {
            let when = self.variants.iter().map(|(name, present)| format!("{} {}", name.trim_start_matches("$intentPresent:").trim_start_matches("$intentSearch:"), if *present { "present" } else { "absent" })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
            selection = format!("when {when}: {selection}");
        }
        self.selections.entry(site.into()).and_modify(|prior| { if !prior.contains(&selection) { prior.push_str("; "); prior.push_str(&selection); } }).or_insert(selection);
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#10 — a covered total unique key reads one row; additional named equalities check that row.
    pub(super) fn read_value(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        for key in &read.keys { if let Some(field) = &key.column { super::super::row_views::predicate_field(info, field, &read.site)?; } }
        if !read.seeks.is_empty() { return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek needs a bounded page", "t[prefix, col >= start] first 50 by index")); }
        let mut point = read.clone();
        if point.keys.is_empty() && info.maintained.as_ref().is_some_and(|maintain| maintain.by.is_empty()) {
            point.keys.push(ast::KeyPart { column: None, value: A::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(true), site: read.site.clone() }, site: read.site.clone() });
            point.index = Some("byGroup".into());
        } else if point.keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{}[] reads nothing; a point read needs a key", read.table), format!("{}[id]", read.table)));
        }
        let implicit_group = mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && point.index.is_none();
        if implicit_group { point.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
        let (mut keys, index, mut selection) = self.select_index(info, &point, mode, out)?;
        super::super::point_key::check(info, &index, read, &keys.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        if implicit_group { selection = "complete maintained group key".into(); }
        let extra = keys.len() > index.columns.len();
        let mut equalities = Vec::new();
        if extra {
            for (column, value, ty) in &mut keys {
                let column = column.as_ref().expect("named key");
                if column != "id" && !info.columns.contains_key(column) {
                    return Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{} has no column {column}", read.table), name_rewrite(column, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)));
                }
                let expected = self.key_ty(info, &index, column);
                if !ty.comparable(&expected) { return Err(type_error(&read.site, format!("{column} is {}; {}.{column} is {}", ty.name(), read.table, expected.name()), format!("use {} for {column}", expected.name()))); }
                *value = self.snapshot(value.clone(), out);
                if !index.columns.contains(column) { equalities.push((column.clone(), value.clone())); }
            }
            keys.retain(|(column, _, _)| index.columns.contains(column.as_ref().unwrap()));
        }
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, keys)?;
        let location = self.site();
        self.record_selection(&location, read, &index, if mode == ReadMode::Exists { "exists" } else if info.maintained.is_some() { "scalar" } else { "row or null" }, &selection);
        self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (read.site.clone(), print::read_expr(read)));
        let mut point = Read { site: location, table: read.table.clone(), index: index.name, key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape };
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.4 — only a caller-proved existence read may use the whole owner partition.
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists && equalities.is_empty() {
            super::super::own_keys::existence(self.schema, &mut point);
        }
        if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(if mode == ReadMode::Exists { Expr::Exists(point) } else { Expr::Get(point) }); }
        let row = self.temp("probe");
        out.push(Stmt::Let { name: row.clone(), value: Expr::Get(point) });
        let mut condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::var(&row)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied aggregate carries a null value, not a present group.
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists && info.maintained.is_some() {
            let present = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::field(ir::var(&row), "value")), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) };
            condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left: Box::new(condition), right: Box::new(present) };
        }
        for (column, value) in equalities {
            let test = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::field(ir::var(&row), &column)), right: Box::new(value) };
            condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left: Box::new(condition), right: Box::new(test) };
        }
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists { return Ok(condition); }
        out.push(Stmt::If { condition, then_body: Vec::new(), else_body: vec![Stmt::Assign { name: row.clone(), value: Expr::Literal(Value::Null) }] });
        Ok(ir::var(&row))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.3 — a seek constrains the first index column after the pinned prefix.
    pub(super) fn seek(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Option<ir::Range>> {
        if read.seeks.is_empty() { return Ok(None); }
        let mut range = ir::Range { gt: None, gte: None, lt: None, lte: None };
        for bound in &read.seeks {
            if index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) != Some(&bound.column) || read.matching.is_some() {
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek compares the first unpinned column of a plain index", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
            }
            let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column);
            let (value, ty) = self.field_value(&ObjectField { spread: false, name: bound.column.clone(), value: Some(bound.value.clone()), site: bound.site.clone() }, &expected, out)?;
            if !ty.comparable(&expected) || ty.is_optional() {
                let endpoint = print::expression(&bound.value);
                let rewrite = if ty.is_optional() {
                    format!("put the read inside `if {endpoint} != null:`, then `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                } else {
                    format!("use a present {} endpoint in `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", expected.required().name(), read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                };
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, format!("seek on {}.{} expects present {}, got {}", read.table, bound.column, expected.required().name(), ty.name()), rewrite));
            }
            let slot = match bound.op.as_str() { ">" => &mut range.gt, ">=" => &mut range.gte, "<" => &mut range.lt, "<=" => &mut range.lte, _ => unreachable!("parser seek comparison") };
            if slot.is_some() { return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek bound is repeated", "write one lower and one upper bound")); }
            *slot = Some(Box::new(self.snapshot(value, out)));
        }
        if (range.gt.is_some() && range.gte.is_some()) || (range.lt.is_some() && range.lte.is_some()) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek has at most one lower and one upper bound", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
        }
        // Null sorts below every present value. An upper-only nullable seek
        // must skip the entire null segment, including its id suffixes.
        if range.gt.is_none() && range.gte.is_none()
            && read.seeks.first().is_some_and(|bound| self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column).is_optional()) {
            range.gt = Some(Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)));
        }
        Ok(Some(range))
    }
}

impl Binder<'_> {
    pub(super) fn variant(&self) -> Option<ir::VariantId> {
        (!self.variants.is_empty()).then(|| {
            let hash = snapback4_core::value::digest(&self.variants);
            std::array::from_fn(|i| u32::from_str_radix(&hash[i * 8..i * 8 + 8], 16).expect("hex digest"))
        })
    }

            }
            A::Object { fields, .. } => self.object(fields, out),
            A::Unary { op, value, .. } => {
                let (e, t) = self.expr(value, out)?;
                if op == "not" {
                    if !t.is_bool() {
                        let operand = print::expression(value);
                        return Err(type_error(site, format!("not needs a bool, got {} — `not` binds tighter than a comparison, so `not {operand} = x` reads as `(not {operand}) = x`", t.name()),
                            format!("write `{operand} != x`, or parenthesize: `not ({operand} = x)`")));
                    }
                    Ok((Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Not, value: Box::new(e) }, Ty::Bool))
                } else {
                    if !t.is_numeric() {
                        return Err(type_error(site, format!("unary - needs a present number, got {}", t.name()), "negate an int, decimal or money value"));
                    }
                    Ok((Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Neg, value: Box::new(e) }, t))
                }
            }
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => self.binary(op, left, right, site, out),
            A::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => self.conditional(then, condition, otherwise, out),
            A::Member { base, name, .. } => {
                // Generated maintain keys are literal path-named columns. Resolve
                // the full chain before ordinary member/reference traversal.
                if let Some((root, path)) = aggregate_path(expr) {
                    let (e, t) = self.computation(root, out)?;
                    if let Ty::Row(table) = t.required()
                        && let Some(info) = self.catalog.tables.get(&table)
                        && info.maintained.is_some() && info.columns.contains_key(&path)
                    {
                        let (value, ty) = self.member(e, t, &path, site)?;
                        return Ok((value, self.field_refinement(expr).unwrap_or(ty)));
                    }
                }
                let (e, t) = self.computation(base, out)?;
                if name == "length" && matches!(t.required(), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::List(..) | Ty::Page(..) | Ty::Collection(..)) {
                    return Err(unsupported(site, ".length", format!("count({})", print::expression(base))));
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — a required reference's field reads its row:
                // `x.ref.col` is `t[x.ref].col`, the same authorized point read
                // (null when the row is missing or hidden), bound once.
                // A recorded attribution column spelled `ref.col` names the value
                // copied at insert; the live read would be a second meaning.
                let recorded = matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Member { name: column, .. }
                    if self.catalog.tables.values().any(|info| info.recorded.contains_key(&format!("{column}.{name}"))));
                if let Ty::Ref(table) = &t && name != "id" && !recorded
                    && self.catalog.tables.get(table).is_some_and(|info| info.maintained.is_none() && info.decl.ephemeral.is_none())
                {
                    let local = self.temp("reference");
                    let runtime = self.declare(&local, t.clone());
                    out.push(Stmt::Const { name: runtime, value: e });
                    let key = ast::KeyPart { column: None, value: A::Name { name: local, site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() };
                    let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.clone(), keys: vec![key], index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
                    return self.computation(&A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Read(read)), name: name.clone(), site: site.clone() }, out);
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.7 — `x.ref.col` reads one reference. A second hop
                // (`r.planting.bed.owner`) gets the two steps that compile, by name.
                if let Ty::Ref(target) = t.required() && t.is_optional() && name != "id"
                    && let Some(rewrite) = self.second_hop_rewrite(base, &target, name) {
                    return Err(type_error(site, format!("`{}` reads two references; `x.ref.col` reads one ({} may be missing or hidden)", print::expression(expr), print::expression(base)), rewrite).guide("shapes"));
                }
                let (value, ty) = self.member(e, t, name, site)?;
                Ok((value, self.field_refinement(expr).unwrap_or(ty)))
            }
            A::Call { name, args, .. } => {
                let (value, ty) = self.call(name, args, site, out)?;
                self.record_call(expr, &ty);
                Ok((value, self.call_refinement(expr).unwrap_or(ty)))
            },
            A::Read(read) if read.table == "connected" && !self.catalog.tables.contains_key("connected") => self.connected(read, out),
            A::Read(read) => {
                let (value, ty) = self.read(read, out)?;
                Ok((value, if self.point_present(read) { ty.required() } else { ty }))
            },
            A::Merge(merge) => self.merge(merge, out),
            A::Exists { read, .. } if read.table == "connected" && !self.catalog.tables.contains_key("connected") => {
                let (count, _) = self.connected(read, out)?;
                Ok((Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Gt, left: Box::new(count), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0))) }, Ty::Bool))
            }
            A::Exists { read, .. } => {
                self.exists_read(read, out)
            }
            A::Created { .. } => Err(unknown(site, "created is valid only in an insert table rule", "use exists here")),
            A::Assert { value, code, message, .. } => {
                validate_code(site, code)?;
                let (mut e, t) = self.expr(value, out)?;
                // A row point may return null because its read meter cut. Do
                // not turn that unavailable fact into an authored NOT_FOUND.
                if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && t.is_optional() && matches!(t.required(), Ty::Row(_)) {
                    e = Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::RequireUncapped, args: vec![e] };
                }
                let tmp = self.temp("present");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: e });
                out.push(Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::var(&tmp)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) }, code: code.clone(), site: Some(site.to_string()), message: message.clone() });
                if let A::Read(read) = value.as_ref() { self.narrow_point(read); }
                Ok((ir::var(&tmp), t.required()))
            }
//! Expressions: precedence climbing over Quarry's operators, the postfix
//! forms (reads, ranges, shapes, presence assertions), merges, writes, walks
//! and the refusals for forms Snapback 4 does not have.

use super::*;

fn optional_filter_equalities(value: &Expr) -> Option<Vec<crate::diag::Site>> {
    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value else { return None; };
    if op != "or" { return None; }
    for (guard, predicate) in [(left, right), (right, left)] {
        let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site } = guard.as_ref() else { continue; };
        if op != "=" { continue; }
        let name = match (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) {
            (Expr::Name { name, .. }, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. }) |
            (Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. }, Expr::Name { name, .. }) => name,
            _ => continue,
        };
        let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site: predicate_site } = predicate.as_ref() else { continue; };
        if !matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "in" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=") { continue; }
        let column = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Member { base, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }));
        if (column(left) && matches!(right.as_ref(), Expr::Name { name: value, .. } if value == name))
            || (column(right) && matches!(left.as_ref(), Expr::Name { name: value, .. } if value == name)) {
            return Some(vec![site.clone(), predicate_site.clone()]);
        }
    }
    None
}

impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn expression(&mut self, minimum: u8, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let start = self.position;
        let mut left = self.prefix(stops)?;
        loop {
            let token = self.peek().clone();
            if self.is_stop(&token, stops) {
                break;
            }
            let Some(precedence) = precedence(&token.value) else { break };
            if precedence < minimum {
                break;
            }
            self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
            self.position += 1;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.3 — recover through guessed equality without admitting an alias or cascading through its block.
            let adjacent_equals = |next: &Token, col| next.kind == Kind::Symbol && next.value == "=" && next.site.line == token.site.line && next.site.col == col;
            if token.kind == Kind::Symbol && token.value == "=" && adjacent_equals(self.peek(), token.site.col + 1)
                && !adjacent_equals(self.peek_at(1), token.site.col + 2) {
                self.diagnostics.push(syntax_with(&token.site, "equality is = in Quarry", "=").mechanical());
                self.position += 1;
            }
            let right = self.expression(precedence + u8::from(token.value != "??"), stops)?;
            if precedence == 4 {
                self.comparison_spans.insert((token.site.line, token.site.col), start..self.position);
            }
            left = Expr::Binary { op: token.value, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right), site: token.site };
            // Optional-filter guards admit the SQL/host-language equality
            // spellings locally; unrelated guessed == retains its repair.
            if let Some(sites) = optional_filter_equalities(&left) {
                self.diagnostics.retain(|d| !(d.message == "equality is = in Quarry" && sites.contains(&d.site)));
            }
        }
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("?") {
            let site = self.take("?")?.site;
            let yes = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let no = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            return Err(unsupported(&site, "? :", format!("{} if {} else {}", print::expression(&yes), print::expression(&left), print::expression(&no))));
        }
        // `a if cond else b`, below every operator: the value is one of the
        // two, and the other is never evaluated.
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("if") && !stops.contains(&"if") && self.peek_at(1).value != "absent" {
            let site = self.take("if")?.site;
            let mut inner: Vec<&str> = stops.to_vec();
            inner.push("else");
            let condition = self.expression(0, &inner)?;
            if !self.maybe("else") {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a conditional value takes both arms: value if condition else other", format!("{} if {} else null", print::expression(&left), print::expression(&condition))));
            }
            let otherwise = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            left = Expr::If { then: Box::new(left), condition: Box::new(condition), otherwise: Box::new(otherwise), site };
        }
        self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
        self.document.source.record(&left, &self.source, &self.tokens[start..self.position]);
        Ok(left)
    }

    pub(super) fn prefix(&mut self, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let start = self.position;
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        if token.kind == Kind::Str {

impl Ty {
    /// A reassigned allocation is bounded by the heap meter, not its first literal's length.
    pub(crate) fn rebound(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Text(_) => Ty::Text(u32::MAX),
            // A known empty value stays empty, even at a loop header.
            Ty::List(_, 0) | Ty::Collection(_, 0) => self,
            Ty::List(item, _) => Ty::List(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Collection(item, _) => Ty::Collection(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Object(fields) => Ty::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowShape(fields) => Ty::RowShape(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowObject(fields) => Ty::RowObject(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.rebound().optional(),
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn optional(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null | Ty::Unknown => self,
            other => Ty::Optional(Box::new(other)),
        }
    }

    fn ordered_call(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {

    fn call_facts(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, args: &[Expr], known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        let mut out: BTreeSet<_> = known.intersection(global).cloned().collect();

    /// Whether two values may meet in `=`, `!=` or a key position.
    pub fn comparable(&self, other: &Ty) -> bool {
        let a = self.required();
        let b = other.required();
        a == b || a.assignable_to(&b) || b.assignable_to(&a) || a == Ty::Null || b == Ty::Null
    }

    /// The type both branches can share. Row-kind conflicts are errors at
    /// their first join, including inside objects and collections.
    pub fn join(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, false)
    }

    /// Nonempty list items must never erase a conflict into Unknown: a later
    /// item could otherwise recover a type the earlier values do not have.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — literals and concatenation share sound item joins.
    pub(crate) fn join_item(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, true)
    }

    fn join_inner(&self, other: &Ty, strict: bool) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        if self == other { return Ok(self.clone()); }
        // Absence adds nullability; it supplies no competing wildcard kind.
        if *self == Ty::Null { return Ok(other.clone().optional()); }
        if *other == Ty::Null { return Ok(self.clone().optional()); }
        if let Ty::Optional(a) = self { return Ok(a.join_inner(&other.required(), strict)?.optional()); }
        if let Ty::Optional(b) = other { return Ok(self.required().join_inner(b, strict)?.optional()); }
        let conflict = || JoinError { left: self.kind_name(), right: other.kind_name(), path: String::new(), incompatible_items: false,
            mixed_references: matches!((self, other), (Ty::Ref(a), Ty::Ref(b)) if a != b),
            enum_items: matches!(self, Ty::Enum(_)) || matches!(other, Ty::Enum(_)) };
        Ok(match (self, other) {
            (Ty::Null, other) | (other, Ty::Null) => other.clone().optional(),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
    }

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

- **[P2] Successful nested positional reads trigger exponential binder work.** [bind/program.rs:597](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r6/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:597) binds every single-candidate operand on a clone, then [line 637](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r6/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:637) binds it again normally. Nested valid operands such as `items[(items[id] ! else NOT_FOUND).id]` recursively repeat both passes: depth n requires approximately 2ⁿ bindings. This makes small accepted programs impractical to compile and contradicts the intended restriction of discarded inference to invalid operands. Preserve the legacy successful binding once, perform diagnostic inference only after failure, and add a nested-key scaling regression alongside the IR-preservation checks.

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

- **[P2] Successful nested positional reads trigger exponential binder work.** [bind/program.rs:597](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r6/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:597) binds every single-candidate operand on a clone, then [line 637](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r6/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:637) binds it again normally. Nested valid operands such as `items[(items[id] ! else NOT_FOUND).id]` recursively repeat both passes: depth n requires approximately 2ⁿ bindings. This makes small accepted programs impractical to compile and contradicts the intended restriction of discarded inference to invalid operands. Preserve the legacy successful binding once, perform diagnostic inference only after failure, and add a nested-key scaling regression alongside the IR-preservation checks.
